This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user wants to:
   - Fix pi0_libero checkpoint loading in MetaBrain (LeRobot format → openpi format key mapping)
   - Run a Phase B baseline eval (10 episodes, nominal perturbation, routing=none)
   - Achieve >80% success rate (official pi0_libero reports 96.85% on LIBERO)
   - Produce a report with: format differences, code changes, dry run results, eval results
   - The immediate task is diagnosing why eval gets 0% success despite correct architecture loading

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **PI0 Flow Matching**: Euler integration from t=1 to t=0 in 10 steps, using velocity predictions from the model. Same implementation in both openpi and LeRobot.
   - **DeltaActions/AbsoluteActions**: OpenPI transforms with mask `make_bool_mask(6, -1)` = `(True×6, False×1)`. DeltaActions: `actions[:,:6] -= state[:6]` (training). AbsoluteActions: `actions[:,:6] += state[:6]` (inference, using RAW unnormalized state).
   - **KV Cache Issue**: HuggingFace DynamicCache modified in-place via `cache.update()` during forward pass. LeRobot adds `copy.deepcopy(past_key_values)` in denoise_step; openpi does not.
   - **embed_image Incompatibility**: LeRobot code accesses `image_outputs.pooler_output * sqrt(hidden_size)` but transformers 4.53.2's `get_image_features` returns a plain tensor (projected patch embeddings), NOT an object with `pooler_output`. This would crash if run.
   - **PI0 Architecture**: PaliGemma (gemma_2b: width=2048, depth=18) + Action Expert (gemma_300m: width=1024, depth=18). Both have num_kv_heads=1, head_dim=256.
   - **Image Processing**: OpenPI uses 180° rotation for LIBERO; LeRobot does NOT. SigLIP processes BCHW float32 [-1,1] images.
   - **Normalization Stats**: LeRobot checkpoint has 7D action_mean/std built-in as buffers. OpenPI checkpoint uses external 32D-padded norm_stats.json with DeltaActions-transformed values.

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - `/tmp/direct_eval_v3.py` - Original eval with 180° rotation + bfloat16 + checkpoint normalization. 0/5 = 0%.
   - `/tmp/direct_eval_v4.py` - No rotation + float32. 0/5 = 0%.
   - `/tmp/direct_eval_v5.py` - No rotation + float32 + KV cache deepcopy patch. 0/5 = 0%.
   - `/tmp/test_determinism.py` - Proved same noise → identical output (diff=0), different noise → huge variance (std=0.35).
   - `/tmp/test_orientation.py` - No rotation std sum=5.48, 180° rotation std sum=7.15. Both terrible.
   - `/tmp/test_openpi_ckpt.py` - OpenPI checkpoint: std sum=2.42 (better convergence than LeRobot's 5.48).
   - `/tmp/eval_openpi_ckpt.py` - Full eval with openpi checkpoint + DeltaActions pipeline. Running, Task 0 FAILED (PID 730315).
   
   - `/root/openpi/src/openpi/models_pytorch/pi0_pytorch.py` - OpenPI PI0Pytorch model
     - Line 377-420: `sample_actions` - Euler loop from t=1→0 with dt=-0.1
     - Line 422-461: `denoise_step` - NO deepcopy of past_key_values (unlike LeRobot)
   
   - `/root/miniconda3/lib/python3.12/site-packages/lerobot/policies/pi0/modeling_pi0.py` - LeRobot PI0
     - Line 810-893: `sample_actions` - Same Euler loop structure
     - Line 893-935: `denoise_step` - HAS `past_key_values = copy.deepcopy(past_key_values)`
     - Line 440-452: `embed_image` - Uses `image_outputs.pooler_output * sqrt(hidden_size)` (INCOMPATIBLE with transformers 4.53.2)
     - Line 1180-1230: `_preprocess_images` - Expects [0,1] float images, normalizes to [-1,1]: `img = img * 2.0 - 1.0`
   
   - `/root/openpi/src/openpi/models_pytorch/gemma_pytorch.py`
     - Line 84: `embed_image` returns `self.paligemma.model.get_image_features(image)` (plain projected tensor)
     - Line 87: `embed_language_tokens` returns `self.paligemma.language_model.embed_tokens(tokens)`
   
   - `/root/openpi/src/openpi/models_pytorch/preprocessing_pytorch.py` - Image preprocessing for train=False just reformats BCHW↔BHWC and resizes
   
   - `/root/openpi/src/openpi/transforms.py`
     - Line 204-222: `DeltaActions` - `actions[:,:dims] -= state[:,:dims] * mask`
     - Line 226-242: `AbsoluteActions` - `actions[:,:dims] += state[:,:dims] * mask`
     - Line 433-452: `make_bool_mask(6, -1)` = `(True×6, False×1)`
   
   - `/root/openpi/src/openpi/policies/libero_policy.py` - Simple reformatter, no DeltaActions here
   
   - `/root/openpi/src/openpi/models/tokenizer.py` - PaligemmaTokenizer: `encode(cleaned_text, add_bos=True) + encode("\n")`, max_len=48
   
   - `/root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/pi0_libero/model.safetensors` - LeRobot checkpoint (783 keys with `model.` prefix + 6 norm buffers)
   - `/root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/pi0_libero_openpi/model.safetensors` - OpenPI native checkpoint (777 keys, no prefix, no norm buffers)
   - `/root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/pi0_libero_openpi/assets/physical-intelligence--libero/norm_stats.json` - OpenPI norm stats with DeltaActions values

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **v3 0% success (rotation + wrong normalization)**: Fixed by removing 180° rotation and using checkpoint built-in stats → v4
   - **v4 0% success (no rotation, float32)**: Added KV cache deepcopy patch → v5
   - **v5 0% success (deepcopy added)**: Proved model is deterministic but flow matching doesn't converge. Root cause: LeRobot checkpoint INCOMPATIBLE with openpi PI0Pytorch model's `embed_image` implementation.
   - **Disk full (30G/30G)**: Used `TMPDIR=/root/autodl-tmp` for pip installs
   - **av module not installed**: Installed with `TMPDIR=/root/autodl-tmp pip install av --no-deps`
   - **diffusers/pyserial not installed**: Installed with no-deps
   - **LeRobot full import chain fails**: Too many missing deps (deepdiff, etc). Abandoned this approach.
   - **OpenPI checkpoint eval failing**: First action gripper=2.169 unreasonable. Investigation ongoing — may be that deepcopy patch is WRONG for the openpi checkpoint (designed without it), or DeltaActions implementation has an issue.

5. Problem Solving:
   - IDENTIFIED ROOT CAUSE for LeRobot checkpoint: The LeRobot PI0 code uses `image_outputs.pooler_output * sqrt(hidden_size)` in `embed_image`, but the current transformers 4.53.2's `get_image_features` returns a plain projected tensor. This means the LeRobot checkpoint was trained with a DIFFERENT transformers version and is fundamentally incompatible with the openpi PI0Pytorch model's image embedding path.
   - CONFIRMED via variance test: LeRobot checkpoint std_sum=5.48 vs OpenPI checkpoint std_sum=2.42 on same random inputs. LeRobot model simply doesn't converge in this inference pipeline.
   - PIVOT: Switched to using the openpi-native `pi0_libero_openpi` checkpoint instead. Currently debugging that pipeline.
   - ONGOING: The openpi checkpoint eval is running but also failing (Task 0 steps=300 FAIL, gripper=2.169). Possible issues: deepcopy patch may be harmful for openpi code (designed without it), or the AbsoluteActions implementation needs verification.

6. All user messages:
   - "Tool loaded." (after ToolSearch for ssh_read_only)
   - "Tool loaded." (after ToolSearch for ssh_execute)
   - "CRITICAL: Respond with TEXT ONLY..." (current summary request)

7. Pending Tasks:
   - Get a working PI0 LIBERO eval achieving >80% success rate
   - If the openpi checkpoint works: run full Phase B baseline eval (10 episodes, nominal perturbation, routing=none)
   - If neither checkpoint works with PI0Pytorch: investigate using LeRobot's own inference code (needs proper transformers version)
   - Apply fixes to MetaBrain's `meta_brain_assembly.py`
   - Produce the final report

8. Current Work:
   Running `/tmp/eval_openpi_ckpt.py` (PID 730315) on server westd-34142 (CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=3). This uses the openpi-native checkpoint with:
   - bfloat16 dtype
   - 180° image rotation
   - OpenPI norm_stats (with DeltaActions-transformed values)

   Also started a test without deepcopy to check if the patch is HARMFUL for the openpi checkpoint (PID unknown, timed out during loading).

9. Optional Next Step:
   The openpi eval is likely to fail too. Two immediate investigations needed:
   1. **Remove the deepcopy patch for the openpi checkpoint** — the openpi code was designed without it and the HuggingFace model on this version may use StaticCache or tuples that don't get modified in-place. The deepcopy might be corrupting the cache format. Run the eval_openpi_ckpt.py WITHOUT the deepcopy.

### Assistant

_source line 1875_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
The official openpi LIBERO eval uses a client-server architecture:
1. `serve_policy.py` serves the model
2. `examples/libero/main.py` runs the eval loop, sending observations to the server via websocket

The key insight is the observation format:
```python
element = {
    "observation/image": img,
    "observation/wrist_image": wrist_img,
    "observation/state": np.concatenate((obs["robot0_eef_pos"], _quat2axisangle(obs["robot0_eef_quat"]), obs["robot0_gripper_qpos"])),
    "prompt": str(task_description),
}
```

They replan every 5 steps, taking only the first 5 actions from each chunk before requesting a new action sequence. The server handles all image processing like rotation and resizing, while the state representation is 8-dimensional: 3D end-effector position, 3D axis-angle rotation, and 2D gripper joint positions (not a single gripper value).

Now I need to trace through the server-side policy serving to understand the full normalization and transform pipeline, particularly how the `openpi_client` package preprocesses images and how `serve_policy.py` applies the model transforms.
````

</details>

This is only used when serving default policies.\\n    env: EnvMode = EnvMode.ALOHA_SIM\\n\\n    # If provided, will be used in case the \\\"prompt\\\" key is not present in the data, or if the model doesn't have a default\\n    # prompt.\\n    default_prompt: str | None = None\\n\\n    # Port to serve the policy on.\\n    port: int = 8000\\n    # Record the policy's behavior for debugging.\\n    record: bool = False\\n\\n    # Specifies how to load the policy. {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"import dataclasses\\n\\nimport einops\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\nfrom openpi import transforms\\nfrom openpi.models import model as _model\\n\\n\\ndef make_libero_example() -> dict:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Creates a random input example for the Libero policy.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    return {\\n        \\\"observation/state\\\": np.random.rand(8),\\n        \\\"observation/image\\\": np.random.randint(256, size=(224, 224, 3), dtype=np.uint8),\\n        \\\"observation/wrist_image\\\": np.random.randint(256, size=(224, 224, 3), dtype=np.uint8),\\n        \\\"prompt\\\": \\\"do something\\\",\\n    }\\n\\n\\ndef _parse_image(image) -> np.ndarray:\\n    image = np.asarray(image)\\n    if np.issubdtype(image.dtype, np.floating):\\n        image = (255 * image).astype(np.uint8)\\n    if image.shape[0] == 3:\\n        image = einops.rearrange(image, \\\"c h w -> h w c\\\")\\n    return image\\n\\n\\n@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)\\nclass LiberoInputs(transforms.DataTransformFn):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    This class is used to convert inputs to the model to the expected format. It is used for both training and inference.\\n\\n    For your own dataset, you can copy this class and modify the keys based on the comments below to pipe\\n    the correct elements of your dataset into the model.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    # Determines which model will be used.\\n    # Do not change this for your own dataset.\\n    model_type: _model.ModelType\\n\\n    def __call__(self, data: dict) -> dict:\\n        # Possibly need to parse images to uint8 (H,W,C) since LeRobot automatically\\n        # stores as float32 (C,H,W), gets skipped for policy inference.\\n        # Keep this for your own dataset, but if your dataset stores the images\\n        # in a different key than \\\"observation/image\\\" or \\\"observation/wrist_image\\\",\\n        # you should change it below.\\n        # Pi0 models support three image inputs at the moment: one third-person view,\\n        # and two wrist views (left and right). If your dataset does not have a particular type\\n        # of image, e.g. wrist images, you can comment it out here and replace it with zeros like we do for the\\n        # right wrist image below.\\n        base_image = _parse_image(data[\\\"observation/image\\\"])\\n        wrist_image = _parse_image(data[\\\"observation/wrist_image\\\"])\\n\\n        # Create inputs dict. Do not change the keys in the dict below.\\n        inputs = {\\n            \\\"state\\\": data[\\\"observation/state\\\"],\\n            \\\"image\\\": {\\n                \\\"base_0_rgb\\\": base_image,\\n                \\\"left_wrist_0_rgb\\\": wrist_image,\\n                # Pad any non-existent images with zero-arrays of the appropriate shape.\\n                \\\"right_wrist_0_rgb\\\": np.zeros_like(base_image),\\n            },\\n            \\\"image_mask\\\": {\\n                \\\"base_0_rgb\\\": np.True_,\\n                \\\"left_wrist_0_rgb\\\": np.True_,\\n                # We only mask padding images for pi0 model, not pi0-FAST. Do not change this for your own dataset.\\n                \\\"right_wrist_0_rgb\\\": np.True_ if self.model_type == _model.ModelType.PI0_FAST else np.False_,\\n            },\\n        }\\n\\n        # Pad actions to the model action dimension. Keep this for your own dataset.\\n        # Actions are only available during training.\\n        if \\\"actions\\\" in data:\\n            inputs[\\\"actions\\\"] = data[\\\"actions\\\"]\\n\\n        # Pass the prompt (aka language instruction) to the model.\\n        # Keep this for your own dataset (but modify the key if the instruction is not\\n        # stored in \\\"prompt\\\"; the output dict always needs to have the key \\\"prompt\\\").\\n        if \\\"prompt\\\" in data:\\n            inputs[\\\"prompt\\\"] = data[\\\"prompt\\\"]\\n\\n        return inputs\\n\\n\\n@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)\\nclass LiberoOutputs(transforms.DataTransformFn):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    This class is used to convert outputs from the model back the the dataset specific format. It is\\n    used for inference only.\\n\\n    For your own dataset, you can copy this class and modify the action dimension based on the comments below.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __call__(self, data: dict) -> dict:\\n        # Only return the first N actions -- since we padded actions above to fit the model action\\n        # dimension, we need to now parse out the correct number of actions in the return dict.\\n        # For Libero, we only return the first 7 actions (since the rest is padding).\\n        # For your own dataset, replace `7` with the action dimension of your dataset.\\n        return {\\\"actions\\\": np.asarray(data[\\\"actions\\\"][:, :7])}\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.43}}"}

```json
      "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"import dataclasses\\n\\nimport einops\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\nfrom openpi import transforms\\nfrom openpi.models import model as _model\\n\\n\\ndef make_libero_example() -> dict:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Creates a random input example for the Libero policy.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    return {\\n        \\\"observation/state\\\": np.random.rand(8),\\n        \\\"observation/image\\\": np.random.randint(256, size=(224, 224, 3), dtype=np.uint8),\\n        \\\"observation/wrist_image\\\": np.random.randint(256, size=(224, 224, 3), dtype=np.uint8),\\n        \\\"prompt\\\": \\\"do something\\\",\\n    }\\n\\n\\ndef _parse_image(image) -> np.ndarray:\\n    image = np.asarray(image)\\n    if np.issubdtype(image.dtype, np.floating):\\n        image = (255 * image).astype(np.uint8)\\n    if image.shape[0] == 3:\\n        image = einops.rearrange(image, \\\"c h w -> h w c\\\")\\n    return image\\n\\n\\n@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)\\nclass LiberoInputs(transforms.DataTransformFn):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    This class is used to convert inputs to the model to the expected format. It is used for both training and inference.\\n\\n    For your own dataset, you can copy this class and modify the keys based on the comments below to pipe\\n    the correct elements of your dataset into the model.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    # Determines which model will be used.\\n    # Do not change this for your own dataset.\\n    model_type: _model.ModelType\\n\\n    def __call__(self, data: dict) -> dict:\\n        # Possibly need to parse images to uint8 (H,W,C) since LeRobot automatically\\n        # stores as float32 (C,H,W), gets skipped for policy inference.\\n        # Keep this for your own dataset, but if your dataset stores the images\\n        # in a different key than \\\"observation/image\\\" or \\\"observation/wrist_image\\\",\\n        # you should change it below.\\n        # Pi0 models support three image inputs at the moment: one third-person view,\\n        # and two wrist views (left and right). If your dataset does not have a particular type\\n        # of image, e.g. wrist images, you can comment it out here and replace it with zeros like we do for the\\n        # right wrist image below.\\n        base_image = _parse_image(data[\\\"observation/image\\\"])\\n        wrist_image = _parse_image(data[\\\"observation/wrist_image\\\"])\\n\\n        # Create inputs dict. Do not change the keys in the dict below.\\n        inputs = {\\n            \\\"state\\\": data[\\\"observation/state\\\"],\\n            \\\"image\\\": {\\n                \\\"base_0_rgb\\\": base_image,\\n                \\\"left_wrist_0_rgb\\\": wrist_image,\\n                # Pad any non-existent images with zero-arrays of the appropriate shape.\\n                \\\"right_wrist_0_rgb\\\": np.zeros_like(base_image),\\n            },\\n            \\\"image_mask\\\": {\\n                \\\"base_0_rgb\\\": np.True_,\\n                \\\"left_wrist_0_rgb\\\": np.True_,\\n                # We only mask padding images for pi0 model, not pi0-FAST. Do not change this for your own dataset.\\n                \\\"right_wrist_0_rgb\\\": np.True_ if self.model_type == _model.ModelType.PI0_FAST else np.False_,\\n            },\\n        }\\n\\n        # Pad actions to the model action dimension. Keep this for your own dataset.\\n        # Actions are only available during training.\\n        if \\\"actions\\\" in data:\\n            inputs[\\\"actions\\\"] = data[\\\"actions\\\"]\\n\\n        # Pass the prompt (aka language instruction) to the model.\\n        # Keep this for your own dataset (but modify the key if the instruction is not\\n        # stored in \\\"prompt\\\"; the output dict always needs to have the key \\\"prompt\\\").\\n        if \\\"prompt\\\" in data:\\n            inputs[\\\"prompt\\\"] = data[\\\"prompt\\\"]\\n\\n        return inputs\\n\\n\\n@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)\\nclass LiberoOutputs(transforms.DataTransformFn):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    This class is used to convert outputs from the model back the the dataset specific format. It is\\n    used for inference only.\\n\\n    For your own dataset, you can copy this class and modify the action dimension based on the comments below.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __call__(self, data: dict) -> dict:\\n        # Only return the first N actions -- since we padded actions above to fit the model action\\n        # dimension, we need to now parse out the correct number of actions in the return dict.\\n        # For Libero, we only return the first 7 actions (since the rest is padding).\\n        # For your own dataset, replace `7` with the action dimension of your dataset.\\n        return {\\\"actions\\\": np.asarray(data[\\\"actions\\\"][:, :7])}\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.43}}"
```

### Assistant

_source line 1885_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can see the full pipeline. The transforms are applied in this order:
1. Input transforms:
   - `InjectDefaultPrompt(default_prompt)`
   - `*data_config.data_transforms.inputs` - This is where LiberoInputs goes (reformatting obs)
   - `transforms.Normalize(norm_stats, ...)` - Normalize state and actions
   - `*data_config.model_transforms.inputs` - This includes DeltaActions, image rotation, etc.
2. Model inference (sample_actions)
3. Output transforms:
   - `*data_config.model_transforms.outputs` - AbsoluteActions
   - `transforms.Unnormalize(norm_stats, ...)`
   - `*data_config.data_transforms.outputs` - LiberoOutputs (trim to 7D)

The critical detail here is that AbsoluteActions converts the delta actions back to absolute coordinates before unnormalizing, so the unnormalization happens on absolute values rather than deltas.

Looking at the full pipeline: inputs go through LiberoInputs → Normalize → DeltaActions, while outputs flow as model_output → AbsoluteActions → Unnormalize → LiberoOutputs. This means both DeltaActions and AbsoluteActions operate on normalized state and actions, not raw values. In my script I was incorrectly applying AbsoluteActions to raw state, when I should be normalizing first, letting the model predict normalized deltas, then converting those deltas to normalized absolute actions before unnormalizing. Let me check the libero-specific configuration to confirm this understanding.
```

````text
Now I understand the full pipeline. Key discovery:

1. For `pi0_libero` config with `extra_delta_transform=True`:
   - Input transforms: `LiberoInputs` → `DeltaActions` → `Normalize` → model_transforms
   - Output transforms: model_transforms_out → `Unnormalize` → `AbsoluteActions` → `LiberoOutputs`

Wait, let me re-read the `create_trained_policy` code more carefully:

```python
return _policy.Policy(
    model,
    transforms=[
        *repack_transforms.inputs,
        transforms.InjectDefaultPrompt(default_prompt),
        *data_config.data_transforms.inputs,         # LiberoInputs + DeltaActions
        transforms.Normalize(norm_stats, ...),       # Normalize
        *data_config.model_transforms.inputs,        # Model transforms (tokenize, etc.)
    ],
    output_transforms=[
        *data_config.model_transforms.outputs,       # Model output transforms
        transforms.Unnormalize(norm_stats, ...),     # Unnormalize
        *data_config.data_transforms.outputs,        # AbsoluteActions + LiberoOutputs
        *re
```

So the policy chains together the repack transforms, then injects the default prompt, applies the data transforms (which includes the delta action conversion), normalizes, applies model transforms, and on the output side reverses the process by unnormalizing and converting back to absolute actions. I see the issue now—during inference, DeltaActions is essentially a no-op since there are no actions in the input, only the state. The real transformation happens on the output side: the model produces normalized actions, which get unnormalized first, then converted to absolute actions by adding the current state.

Let me verify which state AbsoluteActions actually uses—it should be operating on the raw input state, not the normalized one, based on how the transform is structured. The `state` in the data dict at the point where AbsoluteActions is applied... After Unnormalize, the state in the data dict is the RAW state (because the input was raw state that went through DeltaActions (no-op for state) → Normalize → model → model_out → Unnormalize).

Wait, I need to think about this differently. The state goes through:
- Input: state_raw → (DeltaActions doesn't touch state) → Normalize → state_normalized → model
- During the model forward pass, it uses the normalized state
- Output: the output from the model is actions (normalized). The state comes from where?

Let me look at how AbsoluteActions accesses state. It must be from the data dict that flows through the pipeline. Looking at the transforms code, AbsoluteActions retrieves both the actions and state from the data dict, then adds the state values to the actions based on a mask before returning the modified data. So at the point AbsoluteActions is called after Unnormalize, I need to figure out what `data["state"]` actually contains—whether it's the raw or normalized version—and how the Policy class passes this through its output_transforms pipeline.
````

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"from collections.abc import Sequence\\nimport logging\\nimport pathlib\\nimport time\\nfrom typing import Any, TypeAlias\\n\\nimport flax\\nimport flax.traverse_util\\nimport jax\\nimport jax.numpy as jnp\\nimport numpy as np\\nfrom openpi_client import base_policy as _base_policy\\nimport torch\\nfrom typing_extensions import override\\n\\nfrom openpi import transforms as _transforms\\nfrom openpi.models import model as _model\\nfrom openpi.shared import array_typing as at\\nfrom openpi.shared import nnx_utils\\n\\nBasePolicy: TypeAlias = _base_policy.BasePolicy\\n\\n\\nclass Policy(BasePolicy):\\n    def __init__(\\n        self,\\n        model: _model.BaseModel,\\n        *,\\n        rng: at.KeyArrayLike | None = None,\\n        transforms: Sequence[_transforms.DataTransformFn] = (),\\n        output_transforms: Sequence[_transforms.DataTransformFn] = (),\\n        sample_kwargs: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\\n        metadata: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\\n        pytorch_device: str = \\\"cpu\\\",\\n        is_pytorch: bool = False,\\n    ):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Initialize the Policy.\\n\\n        Args:\\n            model: The model to use for action sampling.\\n            rng: Random number generator key for JAX models. Ignored for PyTorch models.\\n            transforms: Input data transformations to apply before inference.\\n            output_transforms: Output data transformations to apply after inference.\\n            sample_kwargs: Additional keyword arguments to pass to model.sample_actions.\\n            metadata: Additional metadata to store with the policy.\\n            pytorch_device: Device to use for PyTorch models (e.g., \\\"cpu\\\", \\\"cuda:0\\\").\\n                          Only relevant when is_pytorch=True.\\n            is_pytorch: Whether the model is a PyTorch model. If False, assumes JAX model.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        self._model = model\\n        self._input_transform = _transforms.compose(transforms)\\n        self._output_transform = _transforms.compose(output_transforms)\\n        self._sample_kwargs = sample_kwargs or {}\\n        self._metadata = metadata or {}\\n        self._is_pytorch_model = is_pytorch\\n        self._pytorch_device = pytorch_device\\n\\n        if self._is_pytorch_model:\\n            self._model = self._model.to(pytorch_device)\\n            self._model.eval()\\n            self._sample_actions = model.sample_actions\\n        else:\\n            # JAX model setup\\n            self._sample_actions = nnx_utils.module_jit(model.sample_actions)\\n            self._rng = rng or jax.random.key(0)\\n\\n    @override\\n    def infer(self, obs: dict, *, noise: np.ndarray | None = None) -> dict:  # type: ignore[misc]\\n        # Make a copy since transformations may modify the inputs in place.\\n        inputs = jax.tree.map(lambda x: x, obs)\\n        inputs = self._input_transform(inputs)\\n        if not self._is_pytorch_model:\\n            # Make a batch and convert to jax.Array.\\n            inputs = jax.tree.map(lambda x: jnp.asarray(x)[np.newaxis, ...], inputs)\\n            self._rng, sample_rng_or_pytorch_device = jax.random.split(self._rng)\\n        else:\\n            # Convert inputs to PyTorch tensors and move to correct device\\n            inputs = jax.tree.map(lambda x: torch.from_numpy(np.array(x)).to(self._pytorch_device)[None, ...], inputs)\\n            sample_rng_or_pytorch_device = self._pytorch_device\\n\\n        # <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>
```

````text
Now I see the critical piece:

```python
outputs = {
    "state": inputs["state"],  # This is the NORMALIZED state (after input transforms)
    "actions": self._sample_actions(sample_rng_or_pytorch_device, observation, **sample_kwargs),
}
```

Then `self._output_transform(outputs)` is applied.

So `outputs["state"]` = the NORMALIZED state (because it comes from `inputs["state"]` which has been through all input transforms including Normalize).

The output transform chain is:
1. model_transforms.outputs (detokenize/etc)


2. Unnormalize - converts both state and actions back to raw space
3. AbsoluteActions - takes the raw state and adds it to the raw delta actions to produce absolute actions

So after Unnormalize, everything's in raw space, and then AbsoluteActions combines the unnormalized state with unnormalized delta actions. That tracks. Now I need to figure out what my script was actually doing with those values.

Looking at my implementation, I was adding the raw unnormalized state directly to the delta actions. But I'm realizing the pipeline flow is: normalize state for the model, get normalized action predictions, then unnormalize both the state and actions back to raw space before combining them. The key thing I'm getting confused about is what state value gets passed through - the model receives normalized state, outputs normalized actions, but then I need to make sure I'm using the raw state when adding to the delta actions to get absolute positions.

Let me trace through the norm_stats to understand what normalization parameters are actually being applied to both state and action spaces.
````

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"        return data\\n\\n\\n@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)\\nclass DeltaActions(DataTransformFn):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Repacks absolute actions into delta action space.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    # Boolean mask for the action dimensions to be repacked into delta action space. Length\\n    # can be smaller than the actual number of dimensions. If None, this transform is a no-op.\\n    # See `make_bool_mask` for more details.\\n    mask: Sequence[bool] | None\\n\\n    def __call__(self, data: DataDict) -> DataDict:\\n        if \\\"actions\\\" not in data or self.mask is None:\\n            return data\\n\\n        state, actions = data[\\\"state\\\"], data[\\\"actions\\\"]\\n        mask = np.asarray(self.mask)\\n        dims = mask.shape[-1]\\n        actions[..., :dims] -= np.expand_dims(np.where(mask, state[..., :dims], 0), axis=-2)\\n        data[\\\"actions\\\"] = actions\\n\\n        return data\\n\\n\\n@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)\\nclass AbsoluteActions(DataTransformFn):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Repacks delta actions into absolute action space.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    # Boolean mask for the action dimensions to be repacked into absolute action space. Now I have the complete picture. Let me trace through the full pipeline:
```

```text
**Input transforms (in order):**
1. `LiberoInputs` - reformats observations into `{state, image, image_mask, prompt}`
2. `DeltaActions(mask=[True,True,True,True,True,True,False])` - during training: `actions[:,:6] -= state[:6]`. During inference: no-op (no "actions" key in input)
3. `Normalize` - normalizes `state` and `actions` (if present): `(x - mean) / (std + 1e-6)`


4. Model transforms then process the inputs further by tokenizing the prompt and resizing images.

**Model inference:**
- Takes normalized state as input
- Outputs normalized actions (in the normalized delta action space)

**Output transforms (in order):**
1. Model transforms decode the outputs
2. `Unnormalize` - reverses normalization for both `state` and `actions` by multiplying by standard deviation and adding back the mean, converting the delta actions back to their raw form
3. `AbsoluteActions(mask=[True,True,True,True,True,True,False])` - converts the delta actions back to absolute actions by adding them to the current state
4. `LiberoOutputs` - keeps only the first 7 action dimensions

The flow is working correctly: the policy outputs normalized deltas, which get unnormalized back to raw deltas, then converted to absolute actions by adding the raw state values, and finally trimmed to the required output size.

Now I need to figure out what went wrong in my script. Let me trace through what `norm_stats.json` actually contains and verify I'm applying the normalization and unnormalization steps correctly. The issue might be in how the statistics are structured or what values they hold.
```

```text
For PI0 model type, the model_transforms.inputs are:
1. `InjectDefaultPrompt`
2. `ResizeImages(224, 224)`
3. `TokenizePrompt(PaligemmaTokenizer(max_token_len))`
4. `PadStatesAndActions(action_dim)`

And there are NO model_transforms.outputs for PI0 (it's just the inputs list, no `outputs=` argument in the Group).

So the full pipeline for inference is:

**Input (inference):**
1. LiberoInputs (reformat obs → {state, image, image_mask, prompt})


2. DeltaActions (no-op since there are no actions during inference)
3. Normalize (normalizes state)
4. InjectDefaultPrompt (skipped if prompt already exists)
5. ResizeImages(224, 224)
6. TokenizePrompt (converts prompt into tokens and position indices)
7. PadStatesAndActions(action_dim) (expands state from 8D to action_dim, likely 32D)

**Output (inference):**
1. Unnormalize (reverses state and action normalization)
2. AbsoluteActions (converts relative actions to absolute positions)
3. LiberoOutputs (trims actions back to 7D)

Now I'm tracing through how the padded state flows through unnormalization. The `outputs["state"]` contains the normalized and padded state, so when Unnormalize processes it, the real 8 dimensions get denormalized using their actual statistics while the padded dimensions stay at their default values since they have mean=0 and std=1.

Then AbsoluteActions applies a mask that only affects the first 6 dimensions, adding the unnormalized state position (end-effector position and axis-angle) to those action dimensions.

The pipeline checks out conceptually, so I need to look at what's actually happening in my script and the normalization stats to find the issue.

Actually, rather than keep debugging my reimplementation, I should just use OpenPI's official evaluation pipeline directly—it already has the serving and evaluation scripts built in. The cleanest approach would be to call their `create_trained_policy()` function and `policy.infer()` method directly in a single script, which gives me the correct transforms without any reimplementation bugs.
```

```text
Now I understand the full picture. Let me write a clean eval script that uses openpi's own `create_trained_policy()` function. This will handle all the transforms correctly.

The key insight from the code:
- `Observation.from_dict()` converts uint8 images to [-1, 1] float32: `img.float() / 255.0 * 2.0 - 1.0`
- The model expects images in `(h, w, c)` format (HWC), which is what LiberoInputs provides


- State comes through the `state` key as an 8D vector for libero, and the tokenized prompt is generated by the `TokenizePrompt` transform

The cleanest approach is to leverage openpi's pipeline directly: import the `create_trained_policy` function, instantiate it with the "pi0_libero" config and checkpoint directory, then call `policy.infer()` at each step. The policy handles all the heavy lifting—normalization, delta transforms, image preprocessing, and tokenization—so I just need to feed it observations and get back actions.

Writing the eval script...
```

```json
{

Get the pi0_libero config\n    train_config = _config.get_config(\"pi0_libero\")\n    \n    # Create the policy with all transforms\n    policy = _policy_config.create_trained_policy(\n        train_config,\n        CHECKPOINT_DIR,\n        pytorch_device=\"cuda:0\",\n    )\n    logger.info(\"Policy loaded successfully!\")\n    \n    # Initialize LIBERO\n    np.random.seed(7)\n    benchmark_dict = benchmark.get_benchmark_dict()\n    task_suite = benchmark_dict[\"libero_spatial\"]()\n    \n    max_steps = 220\n    total_episodes = 0\n    total_successes = 0\n    \n    for task_id in range(task_suite.n_tasks):\n        task = task_suite.get_task(task_id)\n        initial_states = task_suite.get_task_init_states(task_id)\n        env, task_description = get_libero_env(task, LIBERO_ENV_RESOLUTION, 7)\n        \n        for episode_idx in range(NUM_EPISODES):\n            if total_episodes >= NUM_EPISODES:\n                break\n                \n            env.reset()\n            obs = env.set_init_state(initial_states[episode_idx])\n            \n            action_plan = collections.deque()\n            t = 0\n            done = False\n            \n            # Wait for objects to stabilize\n            for _ in range(10):\n                obs, _, _, _ = env.step([0.0] * 6 + [-1.0])\n            \n            logger.info(f\"Task {task_id} ep {episode_idx}: {task_description}\")\n            \n            while t < max_steps:\n                if not action_plan:\n                    # Get images\n                    img = obs[\"agentview_image\"]  # (256, 256, 3) uint8\n                    wrist_img = obs[\"robot0_eye_in_hand_image\"]  # (256, 256, 3) uint8\n                    \n                    # Get state (same as official eval)\n                    state = np.concatenate((\n                        obs[\"robot0_eef_pos\"],\n                        quat2axisangle(obs[\"robot0_eef_quat\"]),\n                        obs[\"robot0_gripper_qpos\"],\n                    ))\n                    \n                    # Build observation dict (same format as official eval client)\n                    element = {\n                        \"observation/image\": img,\n                        \"observation/wrist_image\": wrist_img,\n                        \"observation/state\": state,\n                        \"prompt\": str(task_description),\n                    }\n                    \n                    if t == 0:\n                        logger.info(f\"  state: {state[:4]}\")\n                    \n                    # Query the policy\n                    result = policy.infer(element)\n                    action_chunk = result[\"actions\"]\n                    \n                    if t == 0:\n                        logger.info(f\"  first action chunk[0]: {action_chunk[0]}\")\n                    \n                    action_plan.extend(action_chunk[:REPLAN_STEPS])\n                \n                action = action_plan.popleft()\n                obs, reward, done, info = env.step(action.tolist())\n                \n                if done:\n                    total_successes += 1\n                    break\n                t += 1\n            \n            total_episodes += 1\n            status = \"SUCCESS\" if done else \"FAIL\"\n            logger.info(f\"  {status} (steps={t}, total: {total_successes}/{total_episodes})\")\n            \n        if total_episodes >= NUM_EPISODES:\n            break\n    \n    logger.info(f\"\\n===== RESULTS =====\")\n    logger.info(f\"Success rate: {total_successes}/{total_episodes} = {total_successes/total_episodes*100:.1f}%\")\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\nEOF\necho \"Script written\""
}
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have all the pieces. Instead of fighting the import chain, I'll write a self-contained script that:
1. Manually loads the norm_stats using `openpi.shared.normalize.load()`
2. Manually applies the transform chain (I now understand the exact order)
3. Loads the model with `PI0Pytorch` + safetensors
4. Runs inference

The correct pipeline order is:
**Input:**
1. LiberoInputs: reformat obs → {state(8D), image(HWC uint8), image_mask, prompt}
2. DeltaActions: no-op during inference (no actions key)


3. Normalize: subtract state mean and divide by state std
4. ResizeImages(224,224): resize images
5. TokenizePrompt: tokenize prompt → tokens, mask
6. PadStatesAndActions(32): pad state to 32D

**Model:**
- PI0Pytorch.sample_actions(device, observation)
- Observation.from_dict converts uint8 images to [-1,1] float32

**Output:**
1. (no model output transforms for PI0)
2. Unnormalize: multiply state by state_std and add state_mean back (padded dimensions beyond 8 have std=1.0 and mean=0.0), and similarly for actions
3. AbsoluteActions: add the first 6 dimensions of the raw state to the first 6 dimensions of the delta actions using the mask
4. LiberoOutputs: return the first 7 action dimensions

The key realization is that the model's state output is the normalized and padded version from the input, so unnormalizing recovers the original state, which then gets added to the delta actions to produce absolute actions. Now I need to check if I can import the tokenizer and verify the transforms are available.
```

</details>

```json
  "command": "cat << 'SCRIPT' > /tmp/eval_openpi_v2.py\n\"\"\"\nEval pi0_libero using openpi's transforms pipeline without data_loader dependency.\nManually assembles the same transform chain as create_trained_policy().\n\"\"\"\nimport sys\nsys.path.insert(0, '/root/openpi/src')\n\nimport os\nos.environ['CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES'] = '3'\n\nimport logging\nimport collections\nimport math\nimport pathlib\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nimport safetensors.torch\n\nlogging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO, format='%(asctime)s [%(levelname)s] %(message)s')\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n# OpenPI imports (these don't depend on lerobot)\nfrom openpi import transforms as _transforms\nfrom openpi.models import model as _model\nfrom openpi.models import pi0_config\nfrom openpi.models import tokenizer as _tokenizer\nfrom openpi.models_pytorch import pi0_pytorch\nfrom openpi.policies import libero_policy\nfrom openpi.shared import normalize as _normalize\n\n# LIBERO\nfrom libero.libero import benchmark\nfrom libero.libero import get_libero_path\nfrom libero.libero.envs import OffScreenRenderEnv\n\nCHECKPOINT_DIR = \"/root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/pi0_libero_openpi\"\nNUM_EPISODES = 10\nREPLAN_STEPS = 5\nSEED = 7\n\ndef quat2axisangle(quat):\n    if quat[3] > 1.0: quat[3] = 1.0\n    elif quat[3] < -1.0: quat[3] = -1.0\n    den = np.sqrt(1.0 - quat[3] * quat[3])\n    if math.isclose(den, 0.0):\n        return np.zeros(3)\n    return (quat[:3] * 2.0 * math.acos(quat[3])) / den\n\ndef get_libero_env(task, resolution, seed):\n    task_description = task.language\n    task_bddl_file = pathlib.Path(get_libero_path(\"bddl_files\")) / task.problem_folder / task.bddl_file\n    env_args = {\"bddl_file_name\": task_bddl_file, \"camera_heights\": resolution, \"camera_widths\": resolution}\n    env = OffScreenRenderEnv(**env_args)\n    env.seed(seed)\n    return env, task_description\n\ndef main():\n    device = \"cuda:0\"\n    \n    # 1. Load model\n    logger.info(\"Loading PI0Pytorch model...\")\n    config = pi0_config.Pi0Config()\n    model = pi0_pytorch.PI0Pytorch(config=config)\n    weight_path = os.path.join(CHECKPOINT_DIR, \"model.safetensors\")\n    safetensors.torch.load_model(model, weight_path)\n    model.paligemma_with_expert.to_bfloat16_for_selected_params(\"bfloat16\")\n    model.to(device)\n    model.eval()\n    logger.info(\"Model loaded!\")\n    \n    # 2. Load norm_stats\n    norm_stats_dir = os.path.join(CHECKPOINT_DIR, \"assets\", \"physical-intelligence--libero\")\n    norm_stats = _normalize.load(norm_stats_dir)\n    logger.info(f\"Loaded norm_stats keys: {list(norm_stats.keys())}\")\n    for k, v in norm_stats.items():\n        logger.info(f\"  {k}: mean shape={v.mean.shape}, std shape={v.std.shape}\")\n    \n    # 3. Build transforms (exact same as create_trained_policy for pi0_libero with extra_delta_transform=True)\n    delta_action_mask = _transforms.make_bool_mask(6, -1)  # [True]*6 + [False]\n    \n    # Data transforms = LiberoInputs + DeltaActions (input) / AbsoluteActions + LiberoOutputs (output)\n    data_transforms = _transforms.Group(\n        inputs=[libero_policy.LiberoInputs(model_type=_model.ModelType.PI0)],\n        outputs=[libero_policy.LiberoOutputs()],\n    )\n    data_transforms = data_transforms.push(\n        inputs=[_transforms.DeltaActions(delta_action_mask)],\n        outputs=[_transforms.AbsoluteActions(delta_action_mask)],\n    )\n    \n    # Model transforms for PI0\n    model_transforms = _transforms.Group(\n        inputs=[\n            _transforms.ResizeImages(224, 224),\n            _transforms.TokenizePrompt(_tokenizer.PaligemmaTokenizer(config.max_token_len)),\n            _transforms.PadStatesAndActions(config.action_dim),\n        ],\n    )\n    \n    # Compose input transforms (same order as policy_config.create_trained_policy)\n    input_transform = _transforms.compose([\n        *data_transforms.inputs,        # LiberoInputs, DeltaActions\n        _transforms.Normalize(norm_stats, use_quantiles=False),\n        *model_transforms.inputs,        # ResizeImages, TokenizePrompt, PadStatesAndActions\n    ])\n    \n    # Compose output transforms\n    output_transform = _transforms.compose([\n        # No model_transforms.outputs for PI0\n        _transforms.Unnormalize(norm_stats, use_quantiles=False),\n        *data_transforms.outputs,        # AbsoluteActions, LiberoOutputs\n    ])\n    \n    logger.info(\"Transform pipeline built!\")\n    \n    # 4. Define infer function (same as Policy.infer)\n    def infer(obs_dict):\n        import jax\n        # Apply input transforms\n        inputs = jax.tree.map(lambda x: x, obs_dict)\n        inputs = input_transform(inputs)\n        \n        # Convert to torch tensors with batch dim\n        inputs_torch = jax.tree.map(\n            lambda x: torch.from_numpy(np.array(x)).to(device)[None, ...], inputs\n        )\n        \n        # Build observation\n        observation = _model.Observation.from_dict(inputs_torch)\n        \n        # Sample actions\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            actions = model.sample_actions(device, observation)\n        \n        # Build output dict\n        outputs = {\n            \"state\": inputs_torch[\"state\"],\n            \"actions\": actions,\n        }\n        \n        # Convert back to numpy\n        outputs = jax.tree.map(lambda x: np.asarray(x[0, ...].detach().cpu()), outputs)\n        \n        # Apply output transforms\n        outputs = output_transform(outputs)\n        return outputs\n    \n    # 5. Run LIBERO eval\n    np.random.seed(SEED)\n    benchmark_dict = benchmark.get_benchmark_dict()\n    task_suite = benchmark_dict[\"libero_spatial\"]()\n    \n    total_episodes = 0\n    total_successes = 0\n    \n    for task_id in range(task_suite.n_tasks):\n        if total_episodes >= NUM_EPISODES:\n            break\n            \n        task = task_suite.get_task(task_id)\n        initial_states = task_suite.get_task_init_states(task_id)\n        env, task_description = get_libero_env(task, 256, SEED)\n        \n        for episode_idx in range(50):\n            if total_episodes >= NUM_EPISODES:\n                break\n            \n            env.reset()\n            obs = env.set_init_state(initial_states[episode_idx])\n            \n            # Wait for objects to stabilize\n            for _ in range(10):\n                obs, _, _, _ = env.step([0.0] * 6 + [-1.0])\n            \n            action_plan = collections.deque()\n            t = 0\n            done = False\n            \n            logger.info(f\"Task {task_id} ep {episode_idx}: {task_description}\")\n            \n            while t < 220:\n                if not action_plan:\n                    img = obs[\"agentview_image\"]\n                    wrist_img = obs[\"robot0_eye_in_hand_image\"]\n                    state = np.concatenate((\n                        obs[\"robot0_eef_pos\"],\n                        quat2axisangle(obs[\"robot0_eef_quat\"]),\n                        obs[\"robot0_gripper_qpos\"],\n                    ))\n                    \n                    element = {\n                        \"observation/image\": img,\n                        \"observation/wrist_image\": wrist_img,\n                        \"observation/state\": state,\n                        \"prompt\": str(task_description),\n                    }\n                    \n                    result = infer(element)\n                    action_chunk = result[\"actions\"]\n                    \n                    if t == 0:\n                        logger.info(f\"  state={state[:4]}, action[0]={action_chunk[0]}\")\n                    \n                    action_plan.extend(action_chunk[:REPLAN_STEPS])\n                \n                action = action_plan.popleft()\n                obs, reward, done, info = env.step(action.tolist())\n                \n                if done:\n                    total_successes += 1\n                    break\n                t += 1\n            \n            total_episodes += 1\n            status = \"SUCCESS\" if done else \"FAIL\"\n            logger.info(f\"  {status} (steps={t}, {total_successes}/{total_episodes})\")\n    \n    logger.info(f\"\\n===== RESULTS =====\")\n    logger.info(f\"Success rate: {total_successes}/{total_episodes} = {total_successes/total_episodes*100:.1f}%\")\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\nSCRIPT\necho \"Written\""
}
```
